ParlaMint-ES_2015-04-15-CD150415.u242	Thank you very much, Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, the motion we have tabled is in two directions: on the one hand, to strengthen the Mobile World Capital Foundation, and on the other hand, to focus on the need for policies that accompany the digital transformation that we are experiencing and that contribute to the development of the digital economy. With regard to the first point of the motion, and as we made clear in our inquiry, we are convinced that having as close a tool at the service of digital transformation as the Mobile World Capital, whose most significant event is the Mobile World Congress, is an asset that we must be able to capitalize even more if it can. Mobile World Capital is an instrument at the service of technological and digital development, with a very important tractor effect on the economic fabric, and also a tractor of the cultural change that this transformation requires, and that allows us to advance in the technological and digital transformation of our economy and of our society. The activity of Mobile World Capital, its vitality through the projects that it promotes and develops, demonstrate, both the existing innovative capacity and the scope and dimension of the technological transformation that we live in, and that it forces us to act and to be able to anticipate this revolution as well. All this was once again evident in the recent World Mobile Congress, which has been a further demonstration of the depth, speed and scope of digital transformation, and of the opportunities it creates for citizens, for businesses and for the whole of our economy. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, we believe that all this forces us to act and manage change, and Mobile World Capital today is one of the most important instruments at our fingertips in the service of change and the momentum of this transformation. Mobile World Capital, as I have demonstrated in my appeal, is a dynamic enterprise ecosystem, a dynamic system of mobile and digital base startups, through the attraction of digital acceleration programs that come from all over the world, a dynamic attraction of talent and human capital, and it certainly contributes to giving more value not only to our startups, but to all of our companies with undeniable economic and social value. And what Mobile World Capital is pursuing is the creation of a hub of innovation and digital mobile knowledge, and contributing with it, among other objectives and from a more strictly economic point of view, to also having a new industrial account. And believe me, ladies and gentlemen, this instrument requires continued support from the various public administrations, parliamentary groups and institutions, and requires resources from the different administrations involved to enable new and different tractor projects to be promoted and also to be consolidated. So far the results show how an ecosystem with a very high innovative and technological value is being created through the technology that is being developed, the startups that are being generated, the companies that participate, and how all this reaches multiple economic sectors and society as a whole. I welcome the Minister's words, in the reply to the question, of qualitative and also quantitative support for Mobile and I thank him, and I hope that the rest of the groups will also be able to support this parliamentary initiative. With regard to the second point of the motion and with regard to the challenges of the transformation to which I referred, as highlighted in the inquiry, we believe and believe that policies and measures are necessary to help companies and society successfully take up the challenges of the technological and digital transformation that we are experiencing. The Government, like the rest of the public administrations, must be a driving force in this change through the measures it promotes and the policies it defines, and that is why we understand and propose in our motion the presentation of a report on an annual basis to generate a constructive debate on the challenges, to make proposals, to adopt measures that must accompany and promote digital transformation, because this transformation, which is structural, ladies and gentlemen, requires a great deal of complicity, also requires a great deal of consensus, and requires the participation and collaboration of the public and private sectors, of the various administrations, and also of Parliament. Taking advantage of the opportunities offered by digital transformation requires designing policies that capture the new interactions of the digital world, requires aligning efforts of the public-private sector, requires betting on education and training, and requires a constant and firm look at Europe, because transformation affects us all and Europe plays a lot in this change. The social and economic impact of this digital revolution is very relevant and involves very profound structural changes in economic and social terms. To our motion the People's Parliamentary Group and the Socialist Parliamentary Group have tabled two amendments, which we are grateful for now. With regard to the amendment tabled by the Group of the European People's Party, ladies and gentlemen, we have come to a compromise which we believe reflects both the objectives of our motion and those of the amendment tabled by the Group of the European People's Party, which allows and allows us to broaden and enrich the text of the motion by making a specific reference to the momentum of industry 4.0. With regard to the amendment tabled by the Socialist Group, we are also grateful for the constructive spirit, but we are sorry to be unable to accept it, because it does not enter as it is written in the spirit of either the inquiry or the motion, and it raises some issues which, while important and we can share because they refer to instruments of impulse of technological innovation, but of a more local nature, are not part, ladies and gentlemen, of this motion. In any case, we hope that you will understand the reasons for our position, and that beyond that the Socialist Group and also the other groups will be able to join in and vote in favour of this motion, because they cannot fail to share the objectives that we have pursued and that are being pursued. I repeat my previous words. This motion must allow us to add and demonstrate a consensus between the different groups to reinforce everything that the Mobile World Capital Foundation represents, and above all to strengthen a consensus on the importance of the challenges of digital transformation and the firm and decisive stake for a digital economy, and for a very relevant economic and social impact. Thank you very much.
